Amazon DocumentDB (with MongoDB compatibility) now supports retryable writes starting with engine version 8.0.2, eliminating the need for additional code changes when enabling this capability.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eWith retryable writes, the MongoDB driver automatically retries certain write operations one time if a transient error occurs. Most current MongoDB-compatible drivers enable retryable writes by default. With this release, applications connecting to Amazon DocumentDB 8.0.2 no longer need to set retryWrites\u003dfalse in the connection string to avoid errors. Retryable writes are supported for all write commands except updateMany and deleteMany, as well as transaction commit and abort commands. On engine versions earlier than 8.0.2, retryable writes remain unsupported and must be disabled.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eRetryable writes are available on Amazon DocumentDB 8.0.2 instance-based and serverless clusters in all regions where Amazon DocumentDB 8.0 is available. To get started with retryable writes, see the \u003ca href\u003d\"https://docs.aws.amazon.com/documentdb/latest/developerguide/retryable-writes.html\"\u003eAmazon DocumentDB developer guide\u003c/a\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n","nosnippet":"0","headlineUrl":"/about-aws/whats-new/2026/09/amazon-documentdb-retryable-writes/","postDateTime":"2026-09-28T08:00:00Z","headline":"Amazon DocumentDB (with MongoDB compatibility) now supports retryable writes"}},"tags":[{"id":"whats-new-v2#year#2026","locale":"en_US","tagNamespaceId":"whats-new-v2#year","name":"2026","description":"description placeholder","dateCreated":"2025-12-05T19:55:40+0000","dateUpdated":"2025-12-05T19:55:40+0000"},{"id":"whats-new-v2#general-products#amazon-documentdb","locale":"en_US","tagNamespaceId":"whats-new-v2#general-products","name":"amazon-documentdb","description":"general:products/amazon-documentdb","dateCreated":"2023-11-09T13:51:20+0000","dateUpdated":"2023-11-09T13:51:20+0000"},{"id":"whats-new-v2#marketing-marchitecture#databases","locale":"en_US","tagNamespaceId":"whats-new-v2#marketing-marchitecture","name":"databases","description":"marketing:marchitecture/databases","dateCreated":"2023-11-07T18:00:12+0000","dateUpdated":"2023-11-07T18:00:12+0000"}]},{"item":{"id":"whats-new-v2#074b4246-bd69-4a1d-9e7e-087d8da0a1ba","locale":"en_US","directoryId":"whats-new-v2","name":"074b4246-bd69-4a1d-9e7e-087d8da0a1ba","dateCreated":"2026-09-29T17:43:55+0000","dateUpdated":"2026-09-29T17:43:55+0000","additionalFields":{"nofollow":"0","noindex":"0","nosnippet":"0","postBody":"\u003cp\u003eAmazon SageMaker Unified Studio now supports two new connection capabilities: (1) Iceberg REST Catalog (IRC) connections for external Apache Iceberg catalogs, and (2) IAM authentication for Amazon DocumentDB. Together, they let data teams connect to a broader set of governed data sources and use credential-less authentication across projects.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cb\u003eIceberg REST Catalog connections.\u003c/b\u003e SageMaker Unified Studio now supports Iceberg REST Catalog (IRC) connections, letting customers connect to external Apache Iceberg catalogs that follow the Iceberg REST specification. Supported catalog types include Snowflake Open Catalog (Polaris), Databricks Unity Catalog, and a Generic IRC type for any other spec-compliant catalog. Once connected, customers can browse the catalog in Data Explorer (list catalog/schema/table, view columns, sample data), read and write it in Visual ETL as a source or append/overwrite sink, and query it from data notebooks. Authentication uses OAuth2 or bearer token, and data access uses vended, short-lived Amazon S3 credentials. The connection lifecycle is fully supported - create, edit, delete, and test connection.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cb\u003eIAM authentication for Amazon DocumentDB.\u003c/b\u003e SageMaker Unified Studio now supports IAM authentication for Amazon DocumentDB connections, so customers can connect to DocumentDB without storing a database username or password in the connection or notebook. The connection authenticates using its own IAM role, which DocumentDB validates via AWS Security Token Service (AWS STS). This requires Amazon DocumentDB 5.0 or later instance-based clusters with TLS enabled. The connection can be used from data notebooks, Data Explorer, Test Connection, and Visual ETL data preview.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eThese connection capabilities are available today in all \u003ca DocumentDB (with MongoDB compatibility) now supports\u0026nbsp;retryable writes, 5 new aggregation stages, and change stream enhancements starting from minor version 8.0.2. This release expands MongoDB API compatibility and improves query performance, making it easier to migrate MongoDB workloads to Amazon DocumentDB without application code changes.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eKey new capabilities in version 8.0.2 include:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e- Retryable Writes — insert, update, and delete operations automatically retry on transient network errors, improving application resilience during failovers.\u003cbr\u003e\n- New aggregation stages — $setWindowFields (with its full set of window operators), $bucketAuto, $facet, $graphLookup, and correlated $lookup, enabling advanced analytics, faceted search, and hierarchical queries.\u003cbr\u003e\n- Change stream enhancements — the $changeStreamSplitLargeEvent stage splits change events larger than the 16 MB limit into fragments, and change streams now emit events for createCollection and createIndex operations.\u003cbr\u003e\n- Query performance improvements — enhancements to the query planner add index-only scans for find and aggregation queries, index scans on $expr predicates, incremental sort, and faster count and countDocuments() operations.\u003cbr\u003e\n- Index management — support for the reIndex command on partial indexes.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eThese capabilities are available starting from Amazon DocumentDB 8.0.2 in all regions where Amazon DocumentDB is available. With the compression setting enabled, all new collections created during a database migration or after upgrading the cluster will be compressed by default. The compression parameter also applies to collections created through insert operations or $out aggregation stage.\u003cbr /\u003e\n\u003cbr /\u003e\nMoreover, customers can now modify compression settings on existing collections using the new CollMod settings, without migrating its documents to a new compressed collection. Global tables replicate your Amazon DynamoDB tables automatically across your choice of AWS Regions to achieve fast, local read and write performance. Customers can use the new FIS action to observe how their application responds to a pause in regional replication, and tune their monitoring and recovery process to improve resiliency and application availability.\u003cbr /\u003e\n\u003cbr /\u003e\nGlobal tables are designed to meet the needs of high availability applications, providing you \u003ca href\u003d\"https://aws.amazon.com/dynamodb/sla/\" target\u003d\"_blank\"\u003e99.999% availability\u003c/a\u003e, increased application resiliency, and improved business continuity. This lets customers test and build confidence that their application responds as intended when resources in a Region are not accessible. With in-place MVU, no new clusters are created in the process and you can continue using the existing cluster endpoints for your applications. \u003c/p\u003e\r\n\u003cp\u003eAmazon DocumentDB 5.0 offers compatibility with MongoDB 5.0 API and several enhancements over earlier Amazon DocumentDB versions including support for document compression, faster indexing with index build status, client-side Field Level Encryption (FLE), text search, partial indexes, vector search, and new aggregate operators. Specifying the read preference to \u0027secondaryPreferred\u0027 routes the read queries from the client to your shard replica instances and write queries to the shard writer instance.\u003cbr\u003e\r\n\u003c/p\u003e\r\n\u003cp\u003eStopping and starting clusters makes it easy and affordable to use Amazon DocumentDB Elastic Clusters for development and test purposes where the cluster is not required to be running all of the time. With partial indexes, developers can create an index on a subset of documents that meet a specific filter criterion. By indexing a subset of data, partial indexes can reduce query times and improve performance during index creation and management.\u003c/p\u003e\r\n\u003cp\u003ePartial indexes require less storage and are more likely to fit in instance memory. Amazon DocumentDB already supports unique, sparse and TTL indexing properties, and with the addition of partial index support, customers can now take advantage of an even more efficient way to query data, manage memory and storage, and improve performance. For example, a query for \u0027In Progress\u0027 orders in an eCommerce application can now use a partial index that only includes documents with the status as \u0027In Progress\u0027 from the Orders collection.\u003c/p\u003e\r\n\u003cp\u003ePartial Indexes are currently supported in Amazon DocumentDB 5.0 instance-based clusters in all \u003ca href\u003d\"https://docs.aws.amazon.com/documentdb/latest/developerguide/regions-and-azs.html#regions-and-azs-availability\" target\u003d\"_blank\" rel\u003d\"noopener\"\u003eregions\u003c/a\u003e where Amazon DocumentDB is available. There are no upfront commitments or additional costs to use partial index, and you only pay for the resulting compute, storage, and IO usage. To get started, please refer to \u003ca href\u003d\"https://docs.aws.amazon.com/documentdb/latest/developerguide/partial-index.html\" target\u003d\"_blank\" rel\u003d\"noopener\"\u003ePartial Index Developer Guide page\u003c/a\u003e.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\r\n","modifiedDate":"2024-02-21T18:01:13Z","headlineUrl":"/about-aws/whats-new/2024/02/amazon-documentdb-mongodb-partial-indexes/","postDateTime":"2024-02-21T08:00:00Z","contentType":"Launch Announcement","headline":"Amazon DocumentDB (with MongoDB compatibility) now supports Partial Indexes","postSummary":"\u003cp\u003e\u003ca href\u003d\"/content/aws.amazon.com/live/en_US/documentdb.html\" target\u003d\"_blank\" rel\u003d\"noopener\"\u003eAmazon DocumentDB (with MongoDB compatibility)\u003c/a\u003e announces support for partial indexes. href\u003d\"https://aws.amazon.com/documentdb/\" target\u003d\"_blank\" rel\u003d\"noopener\"\u003eAmazon DocumentDB\u003c/a\u003e (with MongoDB compatibility) now supports vector search with Hierarchical Navigable Small World (HNSW) index. HNSW index lets you execute vector similarity searches with low latency and produce highly relevant results. Vectors are numerical representations of unstructured data, such as text, created from machine learning (ML) models that help capture the semantic meaning of the underlying data. Vector search for Amazon DocumentDB can store vectors from Amazon Bedrock, Amazon SageMaker, and more. \u003c/p\u003e\r\n\u003cp\u003eWith vector search for Amazon DocumentDB, you can simply set up, operate, and scale databases for your ML, including \u003ca href\u003d\"https://aws.amazon.com/ai/generative-ai/\" target\u003d\"_blank\" rel\u003d\"noopener\"\u003egenerative AI\u003c/a\u003e enabled applications. You no longer have to spend time managing separate vector infrastructure, writing code to connect with another service, and duplicating data from your source database. The vector search capability together with large language models (LLMs) enable you to search the database based on meaning, unlocking a wide range of use cases, including semantic search, product recommendations, personalization, and chatbots.\u003c/p\u003e\r\n\u003cp\u003eVector search for Amazon DocumentDB is available on DocumentDB 5.0 instance-based clusters in all \u003ca href\u003d\"https://docs.aws.amazon.com/documentdb/latest/developerguide/regions-and-azs.html\" target\u003d\"_blank\" rel\u003d\"noopener\"\u003eregions\u003c/a\u003e where Amazon DocumentDB is available. \u003c/p\u003e\r\n\u003cp\u003eYou can get started by launching an Amazon DocumentDB cluster directly from the \u003ca href\u003d\"https://console.aws.amazon.com/console/home\" target\u003d\"_blank\" rel\u003d\"noopener\"\u003eAWS Console\u003c/a\u003e or the \u003ca href\u003d\"https://awscli.amazonaws.com/v2/documentation/api/latest/reference/docdb/index.html\" target\u003d\"_blank\" rel\u003d\"noopener\"\u003eAWS CLI\u003c/a\u003e. HNSW index lets you execute vector similarity searches with low latency and produce highly relevant results. Vectors are numerical representations of unstructured data, such as text, created from machine learning (ML) models that help capture the semantic meaning of the underlying data. Vector search for Amazon DocumentDB can store vectors from Amazon Bedrock, Amazon SageMaker, and more. This includes 7x the performance, 4x the number of compute cores, 2x larger private caches per core, 5x faster memory, and 2x faster floating-point performance per core. Additionally, the AWS Graviton2 processors feature always-on fully encrypted DDR4 memory and 50% faster per core encryption performance. These performance improvements make Graviton2 R6g database instances a great choice for database workloads. This includes 7x the performance, 4x the number of compute cores, 2x larger private caches per core, 5x faster memory, and 2x faster floating-point performance per core. Additionally, the AWS Graviton2 processors feature always-on fully encrypted DDR4 memory and 50% faster per core encryption performance. These performance improvements make Graviton2 R6g database instances a great choice for database workloads. With user-defined roles you can grant users one or more custom roles that determine which operations they are authorized to perform. This release improves on DocumentDB’s RBAC support which was previously limited to built-in roles. For some use cases, the built-in roles are not sufficient and you may need the ability to customize authorization across specific actions and resources. For example, you may wish to grant a user read-only access to a specific collection, and grant read-write access to another collection. href\u003d\"https://aws.amazon.com/documentdb/\" target\u003d\"_blank\"\u003eAmazon DocumentDB (with MongoDB compatibility)\u003c/a\u003e is a fast, scalable, highly available, and fully managed document database service that supports MongoDB workloads. As a document database, Amazon DocumentDB makes it easy to store, query, and index JSON data at scale.\u003c/p\u003e\r\n\u003cp\u003eAmazon DocumentDB now supports \u003ca href\u003d\"https://aws.amazon.com/documentdb/global-clusters/\" target\u003d\"_blank\"\u003eGlobal Clusters\u003c/a\u003e. \u003ca href\u003d\"https://aws.amazon.com/documentdb/global-clusters/\" target\u003d\"_blank\"\u003eGlobal Clusters\u003c/a\u003e is a new feature that provides disaster recovery from region-wide outages and enables low-latency global reads by allowing reads from the nearest Amazon DocumentDB cluster.\u003c/p\u003e\r\n\u003cp\u003eGlobal Clusters uses fast, storage-based replication across regions with latencies typically less than one second, using dedicated infrastructure with no impact to your workload’s performance. In the unlikely event of a regional degradation or outage, one of the secondary regions can be promoted to full read/write capabilities, typically in less than one minute. You can have up-to five secondary regions with global clusters, and each secondary region can have up-to 16 replica instances.\u003c/p\u003e\r\n\u003cp\u003eGlobal clusters is available starting DocumentDB v4.0. You can create a new Global Cluster or add regions to existing clusters with just a few clicks on the \u003ca href\u003d\"https://console.aws.amazon.com/rds/home\" target\u003d\"_blank\"\u003eAmazon DocumentDB Management Console\u003c/a\u003e\u0026nbsp;or download the latest AWS SDK or CLI. href\u003d\"https://aws.amazon.com/documentdb/\" target\u003d\"_blank\"\u003eAmazon DocumentDB (with MongoDB compatibility)\u003c/a\u003e is\u0026nbsp;a fast, scalable, highly available, and fully managed document database service that supports MongoDB workloads. Amazon DocumentDB makes it easy and intuitive to store, query, and index JSON data.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eAmazon DocumentDB continues to increase compatibility with MongoDB and today added support for MongoDB 4.0 compatibility including ACID transactions. With support for MongoDB 4.0 compatibility, you can now migrate MongoDB 4.0 workloads to Amazon DocumentDB and use the same applications, drivers, and tools you already use today with your MongoDB 4.0 database with little or no change. \u003cbr\u003e\n\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eThe following are some of the major features and capabilities that were introduced in Amazon DocumentDB 4.0:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cb\u003eACID Transactions –\u003c/b\u003e Amazon DocumentDB now supports the ability to perform transactions across multiple documents, statements, collections, and databases. Transactions simplify application development by enabling you to perform ACID operations across one or more documents within an Amazon DocumentDB cluster.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cb\u003eChange streams –\u003c/b\u003e You can now open a change stream at the cluster level (client.watch() or mongo.watch()) and the database level (db.watch()). You can also specify a startAtOperationTime to open a change stream cursor, and extend your change stream retention period to 7 days (previously, the limit was 24 hours).\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cb\u003eAWS DMS –\u003c/b\u003e You can now use \u003ca href\u003d\"https://aws.amazon.com/dms/\" target\u003d\"_blank\"\u003eAWS Database Migration Service\u003c/a\u003e (AWS DMS) to migrate your MongoDB 4.0 workloads to Amazon DocumentDB. AWS DMS now supports a MongoDB 4.0 source, Amazon DocumentDB 4.0 target, and an Amazon DocumentDB 3.6 source for performing upgrades between Amazon DocumentDB 3.6 and 4.0.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cb\u003eMonitoring –\u003c/b\u003e With the addition of transactions, you can now monitor your transaction usage with five new \u003ca href\u003d\"https://aws.amazon.com/cloudwatch/\" target\u003d\"_blank\"\u003eAmazon CloudWatch\u003c/a\u003e metrics: TransactionsOpen, TransactionsOpenMax, TransactionsAborted, TransactionsStarted, and TransactionsCommitted, in addition to new fields in currentOp, ServerStatus, and profiler.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cb\u003ePerformance and indexing – \u003c/b\u003eIncluded in this release are multiple performance and indexing improvements: the ability to use an index with the $lookup aggregation stage, find() queries with projections can be served direction from an index (covered query), the ability to use hint() with the findAndModify API, performance optimizations for $addToSet operator, and improvements to reduce overall index sizes.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cb\u003eOperators – \u003c/b\u003eWe have added support for new aggregation operators: $ifNull, $replaceRoot, $setIsSubset, $setInstersection, $setUnion, and $setEquals.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cb\u003eRole based access control (RBAC) – \u003c/b\u003eWith the ListCollection and ListDatabase commands, you can now optionally use the authorizedCollections and authorizedDatabases parameters to allow users to list the collections and databases that they have permission to access without requiring the listCollections and listDatabase roles, respectively. Users can also end their own cursors without requiring the KillCursor role.\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eFor more information on Amazon DocumentDB 4.0 and transactions, please see \u003ca href\u003d\"https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/database/introducing-amazon-documentdb-with-mongodb-compatibility-4-0/\" target\u003d\"_blank\"\u003eIntroducing MongoDB 4.0 compatibility and Transactions in Amazon DocumentDB\u003c/a\u003e.\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eTo learn more about Amazon DocumentDB see the \u003ca href\u003d\"https://aws.amazon.com/documentdb/\" target\u003d\"_blank\"\u003eproduct page\u003c/a\u003e and \u003ca href\u003d\"https://docs.aws.amazon.com/documentdb/latest/developerguide/quick_start_cfn.html\" target\u003d\"_blank\"\u003edocumentation\u003c/a\u003e. href\u003d\"https://aws.amazon.com/documentdb/\"\u003eAmazon DocumentDB (with MongoDB compatibility)\u003c/a\u003e is a fast, scalable, highly available, and fully managed document database service that supports MongoDB workloads. Amazon DocumentDB makes it easy and intuitive to store, query, and index JSON data.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eToday, Amazon DocumentDB added support for copying automatic or manual snapshots across regions. With cross-region snapshot copy you can now copy a snapshot to another region for the purpose of disaster recovery, security, or to populate a development/test environment.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eYou can initiate the copy from the \u003ca href\u003d\"https://console.aws.amazon.com/docdb/home\"\u003eAWS Management Console\u003c/a\u003e or the \u003ca href\u003d\"https://docs.aws.amazon.com/cli/latest/reference/docdb/\"\u003eAWS Command Line Interface (CLI)\u003c/a\u003e. There is no charge for the copy operation itself; you pay only for the data transfer out of the source region and for the data storage in the destination region. Today, Amazon DocumentDB added support for new aggregation string operators ($concat, $substr, $substrBytes, $substrCP, $strcasecmp), an array aggregation operator ($size), an aggregation group accumulator operator ($push), and aggregation stages ($redact and $indexStats) that allow you to compose powerful aggregations over your documents. Additionally, Amazon DocumentDB now supports positional array operators ($[] and $[\u0026lt;identifier\u0026gt;]) for updating elements in an array and hint() for selecting an index.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eTo use the new features, you can \u003ca href\u003d\"https://docs.aws.amazon.com/documentdb/latest/developerguide/getting-started.launch-cluster.html\"\u003ecreate a new Amazon DocumentDB cluster\u003c/a\u003e with just a few clicks in the \u003ca href\u003d\"https://console.aws.amazon.com/docdb/home\"\u003eAmazon DocumentDB Management Console\u003c/a\u003e or you can \u003ca href\u003d\"https://docs.aws.amazon.com/documentdb/latest/developerguide/db-cluster-version-upgrade.html\"\u003eupgrade your existing cluster\u003c/a\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eFor more information, see the AWS Database Blog: \u003ca href\u003d\"/content/aws.amazon.com/live/en_US/blogs/database/new-amazon-documentdb-features-for-aggregations-arrays-and-indexing.html\"\u003eNew
